1	---
2	created: 2026-03-29T21:10:42.273Z
3	title: SDK uploadFile hardcodes GCM — wire CTR encryption for media uploads
4	area: sdk
5	files:
6	  - packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts:88
7	  - packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts:113
8	  - packages/sdk/src/client.ts:691
9	  - apps/web/src/services/streaming-crypto.service.ts:53
10	  - apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts
11	---
12	
13	## Problem
14	
15	The SDK's `uploadFile()` in `packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts` hardcodes `encryptAesGcm` (line 88) and sets `encryptionMode: 'GCM'` (line 113). New file uploads through the SDK always use GCM regardless of file size or MIME type.
16	
17	The `selectEncryptionMode()` function in `apps/web/src/services/streaming-crypto.service.ts` correctly returns `'CTR'` for media files >256KB, but it's only called for the duplicate-file re-encrypt path in `useDropUpload.ts` — never for new uploads through the SDK.
18	
19	As a result, the CTR streaming playback pipeline (Service Worker interception, range-request decryption) never activates for newly uploaded files. The decrypt SW works correctly (verified), but files are never encrypted with CTR so `isStreaming` is always false and the encrypted badge never appears.
20	
21	Discovered during E2E test debugging — the streaming-playback CTR badge test correctly caught this bug.
22	
23	## Solution
24	
25	1. Add `encryptionMode` parameter to `sdkCore.uploadFile()` params (already accepted by `createFileMetadata`)
26	2. When mode is `'CTR'`: use `encryptAesCtr` (already exported from `@cipherbox/crypto`) instead of `encryptAesGcm`, generate CTR IV via `generateCtrIv()`
27	3. Pass `encryptionMode` through `client.uploadFile()` → `sdkCore.uploadFile()` → `createFileMetadata()`
28	4. Caller determines mode: either SDK client checks MIME type + size internally, or the web app's `useDropUpload` passes the mode explicitly
29	5. Un-skip the `streaming-playback.spec.ts` CTR badge test after fix
30

# Roadmap: CipherBox v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure

## Overview

CipherBox v1.1 transforms the platform from "IPFS as a storage backend with database fallbacks" to "IPFS-native with the database serving only auth." The milestone establishes performance baselines before making changes, replaces the unreliable delegated-ipfs.dev dependency with self-hosted IPNS resolution, migrates rootFolderKey to an IPFS vault blob (achieving true zero-knowledge server), adds BYO-IPFS node support for data sovereignty, and completes performance baselines with client-side instrumentation and load testing after all features are stable.

## Milestones

- **v0.1 Staging MVP** - Phases 1-10 (shipped 2026-02-11)
- **v1.0 Production** - Phases 11-17.1 (shipped 2026-03-05)
- **v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure** - Phases 18-22 (in progress)

## Phases

**Phase Numbering:**

- Integer phases (1, 2, 3): Planned milestone work
- Decimal phases (2.1, 2.2): Urgent insertions (marked with INSERTED)

Decimal phases appear between their surrounding integers in numeric order.

- [x] **Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation** - Server-side Prometheus histograms and Kubo metrics scraping to establish baselines before any architectural changes (completed 2026-03-07)
- [x] **Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement** - Replace delegated-ipfs.dev with self-hosted Someguy sidecar for reliable IPNS routing, add latency histograms for resolve/publish operations (completed 2026-03-07)
- [x] **Phase 19.2: IPFS Upload Performance Optimization** - Optimize Kubo pinning path (concurrent pins, worker tuning, pin batching) to reduce upload latency (INSERTED) (completed 2026-03-23)
- [x] **Phase 20: Vault Migration** - Move rootFolderKey to IPFS vault blob v2 format, making the server store zero crypto material (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-24)
- [x] **Phase 21: BYO-IPFS Node Support** - User-configurable IPFS pinning endpoint with dual-pin strategy, Settings UI, and connection testing (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 22: Performance Baselines Completion** - Client-side timing instrumentation, end-to-end journey timing, Vitest-based load testing, and capacity documentation (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 23: Rust SDK Extraction** - Extract five Rust crates (crypto, core, api-client, fuse, sdk) mirroring the TypeScript SDK hierarchy, replace duplicated logic in desktop FUSE code, enable unit testing at same granularity as TypeScript (completed 2026-03-24)
- [x] **Phase 24: Bug Fixes & Test Infrastructure** - Fix known bugs (bin IPNS 404, device registry format error) and strengthen test infrastructure (headless load tests, vault recovery E2E, load test auth refresh) (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 25: Desktop Enhancements** - Desktop auto-update mechanism and TEE file enrollment for new files (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 26: Observability & UX Tuning** - Grafana alerting thresholds from existing baselines and timeout tuning for sub-2s UX (completed 2026-03-26)
- [x] **Phase 28: Code Hygiene & Logging** - Structured logger wrapper, replace 124 console.\* calls, fix silenced unpin failures, clean any casts, archive legacy POC (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 29: Infrastructure Hardening** - Wire up IPNS unenrollment on deletion, test login endpoint hardening, IPFS node access control (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 30: Web App Observability** - Error tracking service, error boundaries, client-side telemetry (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 31: Structural Decomposition** - Split monolithic files (useSharedNavigation, FileBrowser, folder.service) into focused modules (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 32: FUSE Async FilePointer Resolution** - Channel-based async resolution to prevent Finder disconnects from blocking FUSE thread (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 33: Windows Async FilePointer Resolution** - Port Phase 32's channel-based async FilePointer resolution to the WinFsp backend (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 34: E2E Test Expansion & Staging Baselines** - Streaming playback, media preview, batch download, and shared teardown E2E tests; BYO-IPFS load test and Faro metrics baselines on staging (completed 2026-03-29)

## Phase Details

### Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation

**Goal**: Operators can observe IPFS/IPNS latency and API performance in Prometheus/Grafana before any architectural changes are made
**Depends on**: Nothing (first phase of milestone)
**Requirements**: PERF-01, PERF-02, PERF-03, PERF-04
**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):

1. Prometheus exposes duration histograms for IPNS resolve, IPNS publish, IPFS pin, and IPFS cat operations with per-operation labels
2. API endpoint response times are captured at p50/p95/p99 for all critical routes (auth, IPNS resolve/publish, file upload/download, folder CRUD)
3. Kubo node health metrics (peer count, bandwidth, datastore size) are visible in Prometheus via scraped Kubo endpoint
4. TEE republish batch duration histogram captures per-batch timing with success/failure labels

**Plans:** 2/2 plans complete

Plans:

- [x] 18-01-PLAN.md -- Add IPFS/IPNS + TEE duration histograms to MetricsService and instrument service/controller timing
- [x] 18-02-PLAN.md -- Add Kubo scrape to Alloy, extend Grafana dashboard, create baseline benchmark script

### Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement

**Goal**: Users experience reliable, fast IPNS resolution without dependency on external delegated-ipfs.dev service
**Depends on**: Phase 18 (baselines must exist to measure improvement)
**Requirements**: IPNS-01, IPNS-02, IPNS-03, IPNS-04
**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):

1. Self-hosted Someguy is deployed alongside Kubo and serves as the IPNS routing provider, with delegated-ipfs.dev fully removed from the resolution path
2. IPNS resolution completes within 2 seconds in the normal case (DB-first with async DHT verification) and degrades gracefully to DB-only when DHT is slow
3. The standalone recovery tool resolves IPNS records via self-hosted Someguy without depending on the CipherBox API or delegated-ipfs.dev
4. No user-visible errors or stale metadata when the DHT is temporarily unreachable (DB fallback serves correct data)
   **Plans**: 2 plans

Plans:

- [x] 19-01-PLAN.md -- Deploy Someguy Docker sidecar, update routing URL in deploy workflow and .env.example
- [x] 19-02-PLAN.md -- Add IPNS resolve/publish latency histograms and instrument IpnsService with timing

### Phase 19.1: Extract core crypto SDK as shared package (INSERTED)

**Goal:** Web app's crypto and file operation logic is extracted into a five-package layered SDK architecture (@cipherbox/crypto, @cipherbox/core, @cipherbox/api-client, @cipherbox/sdk-core, @cipherbox/sdk) enabling load testing, integration testing, and future CLI usage without a browser context
**Requirements**: SDK-01, SDK-02, SDK-03, SDK-04, SDK-05, SDK-06, SDK-07, SDK-08, SDK-09, SDK-10, SDK-11
**Depends on:** Phase 19
**Plans:** 6/6 plans complete

Plans:

- [ ] 19.1-01-PLAN.md -- Split crypto into crypto + core packages with transitional re-exports
- [ ] 19.1-02-PLAN.md -- Expand api-client package with orval generation and configurable instance
- [ ] 19.1-03-PLAN.md -- Create sdk-core with stateless folder/file/IPFS/IPNS operations
- [ ] 19.1-04-PLAN.md -- Create sdk with stateful CipherBoxClient, events, bin, and share operations
- [ ] 19.1-05-PLAN.md -- Rewire web app hooks and stores to use SDK
- [ ] 19.1-06-PLAN.md -- Remove re-exports, update imports, configure Release Please

### Phase 19.2: IPFS Upload Performance Optimization (INSERTED)

**Goal:** Upload operations are measurably faster by optimizing the Kubo IPFS pinning path — the dominant bottleneck consuming ~95% of upload endpoint latency (~1.73s mean per pin, 3 sequential pins per upload)
**Depends on:** Phase 19.1 (SDK needed for load test benchmarking)
**Requirements**: PERF-09
**Success Criteria** (what must be TRUE):

## SDK uploadFile hardcodes GCM — wire CTR encryption for media uploads

**Area:** sdk
**Created:** 2026-03-29 (26m ago)
**Files:**
- `packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts:88`
- `packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts:113`
- `packages/sdk/src/client.ts:691`
- `apps/web/src/services/streaming-crypto.service.ts:53`
- `apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts`

### Problem
The SDK's `uploadFile()` hardcodes `encryptAesGcm` and sets `encryptionMode: 'GCM'`. The `selectEncryptionMode()` function correctly returns `'CTR'` for media files >256KB, but it's only called for the duplicate-file re-encrypt path — never for new uploads through the SDK. So the CTR streaming playback pipeline never activates for newly uploaded files.

### Solution
1. Add `encryptionMode` parameter to `sdkCore.uploadFile()` params
2. When mode is `'CTR'`: use `encryptAesCtr` + `generateCtrIv()` instead of GCM
3. Pass `encryptionMode` through `client.uploadFile()` → `sdkCore.uploadFile()` → `createFileMetadata()`
4. Caller determines mode via MIME type + size check
5. Un-skip the `streaming-playback.spec.ts` CTR badge test after fix

1	/**
2	 * Upload operations - Encrypt and upload files to IPFS
3	 *
4	 * Extracted from: apps/web/src/services/upload.service.ts + file-crypto.service.ts
5	 * Change: Takes raw Uint8Array data instead of File objects (no browser DOM deps).
6	 * Change: Takes explicit SdkContext instead of reading stores.
7	 * Does NOT handle: multi-file orchestration, upload queue, progress UI, quota checking.
8	 */
9	
10	import {
11	  generateFileKey,
12	  generateIv,
13	  encryptAesGcm,
14	  wrapKey,
15	  clearBytes,
16	  bytesToHex,
17	} from '@cipherbox/crypto';
18	import type { SdkContext, TeeKeys, ProgressCallback } from '../types';
19	import { addToIpfs } from '../ipfs';
20	import { createFileMetadata, type FileIpnsRecordPayload } from '../file';
21	import { withPerf } from '../perf';
22	
23	/**
24	 * Result of a single file upload operation.
25	 */
26	export type UploadResult = {
27	  /** IPFS CID of the encrypted file content */
28	  cid: string;
29	  /** Encrypted file size in bytes */
30	  encryptedSize: number;
31	  /** File metadata IPNS name (for FilePointer) */
32	  fileMetaIpnsName: string;
33	  /** File IPNS record payload for batch publish */
34	  ipnsRecord: FileIpnsRecordPayload;
35	  /** ECIES-wrapped IPNS private key (hex) for storage in FilePointer */
36	  ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: string;
37	  /**
38	   * Plaintext file key (AES-256) for post-upload re-wrapping.
39	   * The caller MUST clear this with clearBytes() after use.
40	   * Only present when the caller needs to re-wrap for share recipients.
41	   */
42	  fileKey: Uint8Array;
43	};
44	
45	/**
46	 * Encrypt and upload a file to IPFS, creating file metadata.
47	 *
48	 * This is the core single-file upload pipeline:
49	 * 1. Generate file key + IV
50	 * 2. Encrypt the file content with AES-256-GCM
51	 * 3. Upload encrypted blob to IPFS
52	 * 4. Create per-file IPNS metadata record
53	 *
54	 * Returns everything needed to add a FilePointer to the parent folder.
55	 *
56	 * @param params.data - Raw file content as Uint8Array
57	 * @param params.fileId - Pre-generated UUID for this file
58	 * @param params.mimeType - MIME type of the original file
59	 * @param params.folderKey - Parent folder's decrypted AES-256 key
60	 * @param params.userPublicKey - User's secp256k1 public key for ECIES wrapping
61	 * @param params.ctx - SDK context for API access
62	 * @param params.onProgress - Optional upload progress callback
63	 * @param params.teeKeys - Optional TEE keys for IPNS private key enrollment
64	 */
65	export async function uploadFile(params: {
66	  data: Uint8Array;
67	  fileId: string;
68	  mimeType: string;
69	  folderKey: Uint8Array;
70	  userPublicKey: Uint8Array;
71	  ctx: SdkContext;
72	  onProgress?: ProgressCallback;
73	  teeKeys?: TeeKeys;
74	  /** Override the default addToIpfs pin function. Used by BYO-IPFS to pin directly to user's node. */
75	  pinFn?: (
76	    ctx: SdkContext,
77	    data: Uint8Array,
78	    onProgress?: ProgressCallback
79	  ) => Promise<{ cid: string; size: number }>;
80	}): Promise<UploadResult> {
81	  return withPerf('upload:full', async () => {
82	    // 1. Generate unique file key and IV
83	    const fileKey = generateFileKey();
84	    const iv = generateIv();
85	
86	    try {
87	      // 2. Encrypt with AES-256-GCM
88	      const ciphertext = await encryptAesGcm(params.data, fileKey, iv);
89	
90	      // 3. Wrap file key with user's public key (ECIES)
91	      const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, params.userPublicKey);
92	
93	      // 4. Upload encrypted content to IPFS (or BYO node via pinFn override)
94	      const pinResult = params.pinFn
95	        ? await withPerf('ipfs:upload:byo', () =>
96	            params.pinFn!(params.ctx, ciphertext, params.onProgress)
97	          )
98	        : await addToIpfs(params.ctx, ciphertext, params.onProgress);
99	      const { cid, size: encryptedSize } = pinResult;
100	
101	      // 5. Create per-file IPNS metadata record
102	      const fileMetaResult = await createFileMetadata({
103	        fileId: params.fileId,
104	        cid,
105	        fileKeyEncrypted: bytesToHex(wrappedKey),
106	        fileIv: bytesToHex(iv),
107	        size: params.data.length,
108	        mimeType: params.mimeType,
109	        folderKey: params.folderKey,
110	        userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
111	        ctx: params.ctx,
112	        teeKeys: params.teeKeys,
113	        encryptionMode: 'GCM',
114	      });
115	
116	      // Return a defensive copy of the file key for re-wrapping.
117	      // The caller is responsible for clearing it after use.
118	      const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);
119	
120	      return {
121	        cid,
122	        encryptedSize,
123	        fileMetaIpnsName: fileMetaResult.fileMetaIpnsName,
124	        ipnsRecord: fileMetaResult.ipnsRecord,
125	        ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: fileMetaResult.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted,
126	        fileKey: fileKeyCopy,
127	      };
128	    } finally {
129	      // 6. Clear the internal copy of the key from memory
130	      clearBytes(fileKey);
131	    }
132	  });
133	}
134

42	const AES_CTR_ALGORITHM = 'AES-CTR';

44	/**
45	 * Select encryption mode based on file MIME type and size.
46	 *
47	 * Returns 'CTR' for media files above 256KB (streaming media benefits from
48	 * random-access decryption). All other files use 'GCM' for authenticated encryption.
49	 *
50	 * @param file - File to classify

52	 */
53	export function selectEncryptionMode(file: File): 'GCM' | 'CTR' {
54	  if (STREAMING_MIME_TYPES.has(file.type) && file.size > CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD) {
55	    return 'CTR';
56	  }
57	  return 'GCM';
58	}
59	
60	/**
61	 * Encrypt a file using AES-256-CTR in streaming 1MB chunks.
62	 *
63	 * The file is read in CHUNK_SIZE slices, each encrypted with a counter
64	 * derived from its byte offset. Each crypto.subtle.encrypt call processes
65	 * at most ~1MB, but all encrypted chunks are accumulated in memory before
66	 * being combined into the final ciphertext buffer.
67	 *
68	 * CTR ciphertext is the same size as plaintext (no auth tag overhead).
69	 * Integrity is provided by IPFS content addressing.

2	import { logger } from '../lib/logger';
3	import { useUploadStore } from '../stores/upload.store';
4	import { useQuotaStore } from '../stores/quota.store';

8	import { getSdkClient, hasSdkClient, ensureFolderRegistered } from '../lib/sdk-provider';

11	
12	export const MAX_FILE_SIZE = 100 * 1024 * 1024; // 100MB per FILE-01
13	
14	/**
15	 * Check whether a drag event carries external files from the OS (Finder/Explorer)
16	 * rather than an internal app drag (which sets application/json).
17	 */
18	export function isExternalFileDrag(dataTransfer: DataTransfer): boolean {
19	  return dataTransfer.types.includes('Files') && !dataTransfer.types.includes('application/json');
20	}
21	
22	/**
23	 * Hook for handling external file drops (from Finder/Explorer) anywhere in the app.
24	 *
25	 * Uses the SDK's uploadFile() for atomic encrypt → upload → register flow.
26	 * All IPNS state is managed by the SDK — no dual-path conflicts.
27	 */
28	export function useDropUpload() {
29	  const uploadStatus = useUploadStore((s) => s.status);
30	  const isUploading =
31	    uploadStatus === 'encrypting' || uploadStatus === 'uploading' || uploadStatus === 'registering';
32	
33	  const handleFileDrop = useCallback(async (files: File[], folderId: string): Promise<boolean> => {
34	    // Filter out oversized files
35	    const oversized = files.filter((f) => f.size > MAX_FILE_SIZE);
36	    if (oversized.length > 0) {
37	      useUploadStore
38	        .getState()
39	        .setError(`Files exceed 100MB limit: ${oversized.map((f) => f.name).join(', ')}`);
40	      return false;
41	    }
42	
43	    if (files.length === 0) return false;
44	
45	    // Check quota
46	    const totalSize = files.reduce((sum, f) => sum + f.size, 0);
47	    const quotaStore = useQuotaStore.getState();
48	    if (!quotaStore.canUpload(totalSize)) {
49	      useUploadStore.getState().setError('Not enough storage space for these files');
50	      return false;
51	    }
52	
53	    // Check for duplicates within batch
54	    const batchNames = new Set<string>();
55	    for (const f of files) {
56	      if (batchNames.has(f.name)) {
57	        useUploadStore.getState().setError(`Duplicate file name in selection: ${f.name}`);
58	        return false;
59	      }
60	      batchNames.add(f.name);
61	    }
62	
63	    // Identify which files already exist in the target folder
64	    const folder = useFolderStore.getState().folders[folderId];
65	    const existingByName = new Map<string, string>(); // name → fileId
66	    const existingFolderNames = new Set<string>();
67	    if (folder) {
68	      for (const child of folder.children) {
69	        if (child.type === 'file') {
70	          existingByName.set(child.name, child.id);
71	        } else if (child.type === 'folder') {
72	          existingFolderNames.add(child.name);
73	        }
74	      }
75	    }
76	
77	    // Fail fast on file–folder name collisions
78	    const folderNameConflicts = files.filter((f) => existingFolderNames.has(f.name));
79	    if (folderNameConflicts.length > 0) {
80	      useUploadStore
81	        .getState()
82	        .setError(
83	          `Cannot upload file(s) with the same name as an existing folder: ${folderNameConflicts.map((f) => f.name).join(', ')}`
84	        );
85	      return false;
86	    }
87	
88	    const newFiles = files.filter((f) => !existingByName.has(f.name));
89	    const duplicateFiles = files.filter((f) => existingByName.has(f.name));
90	
91	    if (!hasSdkClient()) {
92	      useUploadStore.getState().setError('SDK not initialized — please log in again');
93	      return false;
94	    }
95	
96	    const client = getSdkClient();
97	
98	    // Resolve parent folder and ensure it's registered in the SDK
99	    const folders = useFolderStore.getState().folders;
100	    const vault = useVaultStore.getState();
101	    const parentFolder =
102	      folderId === 'root' ? getRootFolderState(vault, folders) : folders[folderId];
103	    if (!parentFolder) {
104	      useUploadStore.getState().setError('Parent folder not found');
105	      return false;
106	    }
107	    ensureFolderRegistered(parentFolder);
108	
109	    const uploadStore = useUploadStore.getState();
110	    // Start with all files (new + duplicates) since we encrypt all upfront
111	    uploadStore.startUpload(files.length);
112	
113	    const uploadedCids: string[] = []; // Track for cleanup on failure
114	
115	    try {
116	      // Upload new files via SDK (atomic: encrypt → IPFS → FilePointer → IPNS publish)
117	      for (const file of newFiles) {
118	        if (useUploadStore.getState().status === 'cancelled') {
119	          throw new Error('Upload cancelled by user');
120	        }
121	
122	        useUploadStore.getState().setUploading(file.name, 0);
123	
124	        // Read File to Uint8Array
125	        const data = new Uint8Array(await file.arrayBuffer());
126	
127	        const result = await client.uploadFile(
128	          parentFolder.ipnsName,
129	          data,
130	          file.name,
131	          file.type || 'application/octet-stream',
132	          (percent) => useUploadStore.getState().setUploading(file.name, percent)
133	        );
134	
135	        uploadedCids.push(result.cid);
136	        useUploadStore.getState().fileComplete();
137	      }
138	
139	      // Handle duplicate files: encrypt + upload to IPFS, then surface as pending replacements
140	      // (These don't get registered in the folder — the user decides via the Replace dialog)
141	      // Duplicate files always upload to CipherBox relay (not BYO node) because
142	      // they're staged for the replacement dialog. Once the user confirms, the
143	      // replacement flow uses SDK's uploadFile() which respects pinning mode.
144	      if (duplicateFiles.length > 0) {
145	        const replacements: PendingReplacement[] = [];
146	
147	        // Hoist dynamic imports before the loop to avoid per-iteration async overhead
148	        const { encryptFile } = await import('../services/file-crypto.service');
149	        const { addToIpfs } = await import('../lib/api/ipfs');
150	
151	        for (const file of duplicateFiles) {
152	          if (useUploadStore.getState().status === 'cancelled') {
153	            throw new Error('Upload cancelled by user');
154	          }
155	
156	          useUploadStore.getState().setUploading(file.name, 0);
157	
158	          // For duplicates, we only encrypt + upload to IPFS (don't register in folder)
159	          // Use the old upload service for this since SDK's uploadFile registers in folder
160	          const userPublicKey = useAuthStore.getState().vaultKeypair?.publicKey;
161	          if (!userPublicKey) throw new Error('No keypair available');
162	          const encrypted = await encryptFile(file, userPublicKey);
163	          // Pass a clean Uint8Array copy — never use .buffer (may include extra bytes)
164	          const blob = new Blob([new Uint8Array(encrypted.ciphertext)], {
165	            type: 'application/octet-stream',
166	          });
167	          const ipfsResult = await addToIpfs(blob, (percent) =>
168	            useUploadStore.getState().setUploading(file.name, percent)
169	          );
170	
171	          uploadedCids.push(ipfsResult.cid);
172	          useUploadStore.getState().fileComplete();
173	
174	          const existingFileId = existingByName.get(file.name);
175	          if (existingFileId) {
176	            replacements.push({
177	              fileName: file.name,
178	              fileId: existingFileId,
179	              parentId: folderId,
180	              encryptedData: {
181	                cid: ipfsResult.cid,
182	                wrappedKey: encrypted.wrappedKey,
183	                iv: encrypted.iv,
184	                size: file.size,
185	                encryptionMode: encrypted.encryptionMode,
186	              },
187	            });
188	          }
189	        }
190	
191	        if (replacements.length > 0) {
192	          useUploadStore.getState().setPendingReplacements(replacements);
193	        }
194	      }
195	
196	      useUploadStore.getState().setSuccess();
197	      return true;
198	    } catch (err) {
199	      const message = (err as Error).message;
200	      if (message !== 'Upload cancelled by user') {
201	        useUploadStore.getState().setError(message);
202	      }
203	      // Best-effort cleanup: unpin orphaned CIDs from failed upload
204	      if (uploadedCids.length > 0) {
205	        import('../lib/api/ipfs')
206	          .then(({ unpinFromIpfs }) => {
207	            for (const cid of uploadedCids) {
208	              unpinFromIpfs(cid).catch((err) =>
209	                logger.warn('[Upload] Unpin orphaned CID failed:', err)
210	              );
211	            }
212	          })
213	          .catch((err) => logger.warn('[Upload] Failed to load IPFS module for cleanup:', err));
214	      }
215	      return false;
216	    } finally {
217	      // Refresh quota from server
218	      await useQuotaStore.getState().fetchQuota();
219	    }
220	  }, []);
221	
222	  return { handleFileDrop, isUploading };
223	}

60	 * The 8/8 nonce/counter split with 64-bit counter supports files up to
61	 * 2^64 * 16 bytes (effectively unlimited). The counter starts at zero so
62	 * random-access offset calculation is simply `blockIndex = byteOffset / 16`.
63	 *
64	 * NEVER reuse a nonce with the same key -- CTR nonce reuse is catastrophic.
65	 *
66	 * @returns 16-byte IV with random nonce and zero counter
67	 */
68	export function generateCtrIv(): Uint8Array {
69	  const iv = new Uint8Array(AES_CTR_IV_SIZE);
70	  // Random nonce in first 8 bytes
71	  const nonce = generateRandomBytes(AES_CTR_NONCE_SIZE);
72	  iv.set(nonce, 0);
73	  // Last 8 bytes remain zero (counter starts at 0)
74	  return iv;
75	}

1	/**
2	 * @cipherbox/crypto - AES-256-CTR Encryption
3	 *
4	 * Symmetric encryption for media file content using CTR mode.
5	 * CTR mode enables random-access decryption (any byte range without
6	 * processing preceding bytes), required for Service Worker streaming.
7	 *
8	 * Uses Web Crypto API for hardware-accelerated encryption.
9	 *
10	 * SECURITY NOTE: AES-CTR does NOT provide authentication (unlike GCM).
11	 * The caller is responsible for integrity verification via separate
12	 * mechanisms (e.g., IPFS content addressing provides integrity).
13	 */
14	
15	import { CryptoError } from '../types';
16	import { AES_KEY_SIZE, AES_CTR_IV_SIZE, AES_CTR_ALGORITHM, AES_CTR_LENGTH } from '../constants';
17	
18	/**
19	 * Encrypt data using AES-256-CTR.
20	 *
21	 * Each encryption MUST use a unique IV (nonce + counter) with the same key.
22	 * Reusing nonce+key pairs is catastrophic for AES-CTR security.
23	 *
24	 * CTR output is the same size as the input (no authentication tag).
25	 *
26	 * @param plaintext - Data to encrypt
27	 * @param key - 32-byte AES key
28	 * @param iv - 16-byte counter block (8-byte nonce + 8-byte counter)
29	 * @returns Ciphertext (same size as plaintext)
30	 * @throws CryptoError with generic message on any failure
31	 */
32	export async function encryptAesCtr(
33	  plaintext: Uint8Array,
34	  key: Uint8Array,
35	  iv: Uint8Array
36	): Promise<Uint8Array> {
37	  // Validate key size
38	  if (key.length !== AES_KEY_SIZE) {
39	    throw new CryptoError('Encryption failed', 'INVALID_KEY_SIZE');
40	  }
41	
42	  // Validate IV size
43	  if (iv.length !== AES_CTR_IV_SIZE) {
44	    throw new CryptoError('Encryption failed', 'INVALID_IV_SIZE');
45	  }
46	
47	  try {
48	    // Copy to ensure proper ArrayBuffer (not SharedArrayBuffer)
49	    const keyBuffer = new Uint8Array(key).buffer as ArrayBuffer;
50	    const ivBuffer = new Uint8Array(iv);
51	    const plaintextBuffer = new Uint8Array(plaintext).buffer as ArrayBuffer;
52	
53	    // Import key for encryption
54	    const cryptoKey = await crypto.subtle.importKey(
55	      'raw',
56	      keyBuffer,
57	      { name: AES_CTR_ALGORITHM },
58	      false,
59	      ['encrypt']
60	    );
61	
62	    // Encrypt - CTR output is same size as input (XOR-based stream cipher)
63	    const ciphertext = await crypto.subtle.encrypt(
64	      { name: AES_CTR_ALGORITHM, counter: ivBuffer, length: AES_CTR_LENGTH },
65	      cryptoKey,
66	      plaintextBuffer
67	    );
68	
69	    return new Uint8Array(ciphertext);
70	  } catch {
71	    // Generic error to prevent oracle attacks
72	    throw new CryptoError('Encryption failed', 'ENCRYPTION_FAILED');
73	  }
74	}
75

> AGENT

All done. Here's a summary of the changes:

**`packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts`** — Added optional `encryptionMode` param. When `'CTR'`, uses `encryptAesCtr` + `generateCtrIv` instead of GCM. Passes the mode through to `createFileMetadata`.

**`packages/sdk/src/client.ts`** — Added `selectEncryptionMode()` that checks MIME type (video/mp4, audio/*, etc.) and size (>256KB) to pick CTR for streaming media. Called before `sdkCore.uploadFile()` and passed through.

**`tests/web-e2e/tests/streaming-playback.spec.ts`** — Removed `test.skip` from the CTR encrypted badge test.

**`packages/sdk-core` build output** — Rebuilt to export the new `encryptionMode` param in types.

Want me to commit this?

1	import { test, expect, Browser, BrowserContext, Page } from '@playwright/test';

9	/**
10	 * AES-CTR Streaming Playback E2E Test Suite
11	 *
12	 * Verifies the streaming decryption pipeline:
13	 * - Large video (>256KB) triggers AES-CTR mode via service worker interception
14	 * - Small video (<256KB) falls back to AES-GCM blob URL playback
15	 * - Video player modal renders with controls and encrypted badge
16	 * - Decrypt progress indicator appears during initial load
17	 */
18	test.describe.serial('AES-CTR Streaming Playback', () => {
19	  let browser: Browser;
20	  let context: BrowserContext;
21	  let page: Page;
22	  let fileList: FileListPage;
23	  let uploadZone: UploadZonePage;

28	
29	  test.setTimeout(180_000);
30	
31	  test.beforeAll(async ({ browser: b }) => {
32	    // Extend hook timeout — match suite timeout (default 30s is too short for wallet login)
33	    test.setTimeout(180_000);
34	    browser = b;
35	    const account = createTestAccount();
36	    context = await browser.newContext();
37	    page = await context.newPage();
38	    await setupMockWallet(page, account);
39	    const result = await loginViaWallet(page, { timeout: 90_000 });
40	    expect(result.outcome).toBe('success');
41	    fileList = new FileListPage(page);
42	    uploadZone = new UploadZonePage(page);
43	    contextMenu = new ContextMenuPage(page);
44	  });
45	
46	  test.afterAll(async () => {
47	    cleanupTestFiles();
48	    if (page) await deleteAccountViaPage(page);
49	    if (context) await context.close();
50	  });
51	
52	  test('upload large video file (CTR mode)', async () => {
53	    const file = createTestMediaFile('test-video.mp4', videoName);
54	    await uploadZone.uploadFile(file.path);
55	    await fileList.waitForItemToAppear(videoName, { timeout: 60_000 });
56	    expect(await fileList.isItemVisible(videoName)).toBe(true);
57	  });
58	
59	  test('video preview opens with player controls', async () => {
60	    await fileList.rightClickItem(videoName);
61	    await contextMenu.waitForOpen();
62	    await contextMenu.clickPreview();
63	
64	    // Wait for the video player modal to appear
65	    await page.locator('.video-player-modal').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 });
66	
67	    // Assert video element is present inside the modal
68	    await expect(page.locator('.video-player-modal video')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15_000 });
69	
70	    // Close modal
71	    await page.keyboard.press('Escape');
72	    await page.locator('.video-player-modal').waitFor({ state: 'hidden', timeout: 5_000 });
73	  });
74	
75	  test('CTR encrypted badge visible for large video', async () => {
76	    // Re-open preview
77	    await fileList.rightClickItem(videoName);
78	    await contextMenu.waitForOpen();

80	    await page.locator('.video-player-modal').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 });
81	
82	    // Wait for the encrypted badge indicating AES-CTR streaming mode
83	    await page.locator('.video-cipher-badge').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 });
84	    const badgeText = await page.locator('.video-cipher-badge').textContent();
85	    expect(badgeText?.toUpperCase()).toContain('ENCRYPTED');

87	    // Close modal

92	  test('decrypt progress shows during initial load', async () => {

96	    await contextMenu.clickPreview();
97	    await page.locator('.video-player-modal').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 });
98	
99	    // The progress bar may appear very briefly -- use a soft assertion
100	    const progressFill = page.locator('.video-decrypt-progress-fill');
101	    const appeared = await progressFill
102	      .waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 5_000 })
103	      .then(() => true)
104	      .catch(() => false);
105	
106	    if (appeared) {
107	      // Verify it has a width style (indicating actual progress rendering)
108	      const style = await progressFill.getAttribute('style');

index 0d410f961..000000000
--- a/.planning/todos/pending/2026-03-29-sdk-uploadfile-hardcodes-gcm-wire-ctr-encryption-for-media-uploads.md
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,29 +0,0 @@
----
-created: 2026-03-29T21:10:42.273Z
-title: SDK uploadFile hardcodes GCM — wire CTR encryption for media uploads
-area: sdk
-files:
-  - packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts:88
-  - packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts:113
-  - packages/sdk/src/client.ts:691
-  - apps/web/src/services/streaming-crypto.service.ts:53
-  - apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts
----
-
-## Problem
-
-The SDK's `uploadFile()` in `packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts` hardcodes `encryptAesGcm` (line 88) and sets `encryptionMode: 'GCM'` (line 113). New file uploads through the SDK always use GCM regardless of file size or MIME type.
-
-The `selectEncryptionMode()` function in `apps/web/src/services/streaming-crypto.service.ts` correctly returns `'CTR'` for media files >256KB, but it's only called for the duplicate-file re-encrypt path in `useDropUpload.ts` — never for new uploads through the SDK.
-
-As a result, the CTR streaming playback pipeline (Service Worker interception, range-request decryption) never activates for newly uploaded files. The decrypt SW works correctly (verified), but files are never encrypted with CTR so `isStreaming` is always false and the encrypted badge never appears.
-
-Discovered during E2E test debugging — the streaming-playback CTR badge test correctly caught this bug.
-
-## Solution
-
-1. Add `encryptionMode` parameter to `sdkCore.uploadFile()` params (already accepted by `createFileMetadata`)
-2. When mode is `'CTR'`: use `encryptAesCtr` (already exported from `@cipherbox/crypto`) instead of `encryptAesGcm`, generate CTR IV via `generateCtrIv()`
-3. Pass `encryptionMode` through `client.uploadFile()` → `sdkCore.uploadFile()` → `createFileMetadata()`
-4. Caller determines mode: either SDK client checks MIME type + size internally, or the web app's `useDropUpload` passes the mode explicitly
-5. Un-skip the `streaming-playback.spec.ts` CTR badge test after fix

+
+// ---- Encryption mode selection ----
+
+/** MIME types eligible for CTR streaming encryption */
+const STREAMING_MIME_TYPES = new Set([
+  'video/mp4',
+  'video/webm',
+  'audio/mpeg',
+  'audio/mp4',
+  'audio/webm',
+  'audio/ogg',
+  'audio/aac',
+]);
+
+/** Minimum file size (bytes) to use CTR mode: 256KB */
+const CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD = 256 * 1024;
+
+/**
+ * Select encryption mode based on MIME type and size.
+ * Media files above 256KB use CTR for random-access streaming decryption.
+ */
+function selectEncryptionMode(mimeType: string, size: number): 'GCM' | 'CTR' {
+  if (STREAMING_MIME_TYPES.has(mimeType) && size > CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD) {
+    return 'CTR';
+  }
+  return 'GCM';
+}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk-core/src/encryption-mode.ts",
  "content": "/**\n * Encryption mode selection for file uploads.\n *\n * Centralizes the policy for choosing AES-CTR vs AES-GCM so that\n * all upload paths (SDK client, web app duplicate-file path) use\n * the same MIME type list and size threshold.\n */\n\n/** MIME types eligible for CTR streaming encryption */\nconst STREAMING_MIME_TYPES = new Set([\n  'video/mp4',\n  'video/webm',\n  'audio/mpeg',\n  'audio/mp4',\n  'audio/webm',\n  'audio/ogg',\n  'audio/aac',\n  'audio/flac',\n]);\n\n/** Minimum file size (bytes) to use CTR mode: 256KB */\nconst CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD = 256 * 1024;\n\nexport type EncryptionMode = 'GCM' | 'CTR';\n\n/**\n * Select encryption mode based on MIME type and file size.\n *\n * Media files above 256KB use AES-256-CTR for random-access streaming\n * decryption via Service Worker. All other files use AES-256-GCM for\n * authenticated encryption.\n */\nexport function selectEncryptionMode(mimeType: string, size: number): EncryptionMode {\n  if (STREAMING_MIME_TYPES.has(mimeType) && size > CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD) {\n    return 'CTR';\n  }\n  return 'GCM';\n}\n\n/**\n * Validate and normalize an encryption mode value.\n *\n * Coerces any non-'CTR' value to 'GCM' so that invalid input from\n * JS consumers never propagates into file metadata.\n */\nexport function normalizeEncryptionMode(mode: string | undefined): EncryptionMode {\n  return mode === 'CTR' ? 'CTR' : 'GCM';\n}\n"
}

1	/**
2	 * Streaming Crypto Service - Mode selection and AES-256-CTR streaming encryption
3	 *
4	 * Media files above 256KB are encrypted with AES-256-CTR using streaming 1MB chunks,
5	 * which keeps memory bounded regardless of file size. CTR mode produces ciphertext
6	 * that supports random-access decryption for streaming media playback.
7	 *
8	 * Non-media files and small media files continue using AES-256-GCM via the
9	 * existing file-crypto.service.ts path.
10	 */
11	
12	import {
13	  generateFileKey,
14	  generateCtrIv,
15	  wrapKey,
16	  clearBytes,
17	  bytesToHex,
18	  AES_CTR_LENGTH,
19	  AES_CTR_NONCE_SIZE,
20	} from '@cipherbox/crypto';
21	
22	import type { EncryptedFileResult } from './file-crypto.service';
23	
24	/** MIME types eligible for CTR streaming encryption */
25	const STREAMING_MIME_TYPES = new Set([
26	  'video/mp4',
27	  'video/webm',
28	  'audio/mpeg',
29	  'audio/mp4',
30	  'audio/webm',
31	  'audio/ogg',
32	  'audio/aac',
33	  'audio/flac',
34	]);
35	
36	/** Minimum file size (bytes) to use CTR mode: 256KB */
37	const CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD = 256 * 1024;
38	
39	/** Chunk size for streaming encryption: 1MB */
40	const CHUNK_SIZE = 1024 * 1024;
41	
42	/** AES-CTR algorithm name */
43	const AES_CTR_ALGORITHM = 'AES-CTR';
44	
45	/**
46	 * Select encryption mode based on file MIME type and size.
47	 *
48	 * Returns 'CTR' for media files above 256KB (streaming media benefits from
49	 * random-access decryption). All other files use 'GCM' for authenticated encryption.
50	 *
51	 * @param file - File to classify
52	 * @returns 'CTR' for streaming media, 'GCM' for everything else
53	 */
54	export function selectEncryptionMode(file: File): 'GCM' | 'CTR' {
55	  if (STREAMING_MIME_TYPES.has(file.type) && file.size > CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD) {
56	    return 'CTR';
57	  }
58	  return 'GCM';
59	}
60	
61	/**
62	 * Encrypt a file using AES-256-CTR in streaming 1MB chunks.
63	 *
64	 * The file is read in CHUNK_SIZE slices, each encrypted with a counter
65	 * derived from its byte offset. Each crypto.subtle.encrypt call processes
66	 * at most ~1MB, but all encrypted chunks are accumulated in memory before
67	 * being combined into the final ciphertext buffer.
68	 *
69	 * CTR ciphertext is the same size as plaintext (no auth tag overhead).
70	 * Integrity is provided by IPFS content addressing.
71	 *
72	 * @param file - File to encrypt
73	 * @param userPublicKey - User's secp256k1 public key (65 bytes, uncompressed)
74	 * @returns Encrypted file data with wrapped key and IV
75	 */
76	export async function encryptFileCtr(
77	  file: File,
78	  userPublicKey: Uint8Array
79	): Promise<EncryptedFileResult> {
80	  // 1. Generate unique file key and CTR IV
81	  const fileKey = generateFileKey();
82	  const iv = generateCtrIv();
83	
84	  // 2. Import CryptoKey once (not per-chunk)
85	  const cryptoKey = await crypto.subtle.importKey(
86	    'raw',
87	    fileKey as BufferSource,
88	    { name: AES_CTR_ALGORITHM },
89	    false,
90	    ['encrypt']
91	  );
92	
93	  // 3. Stream-encrypt file in 1MB chunks
94	  const encryptedChunks: Uint8Array[] = [];
95	  let offset = 0;
96	
97	  while (offset < file.size) {
98	    const end = Math.min(offset + CHUNK_SIZE, file.size);
99	    const chunk = new Uint8Array(await file.slice(offset, end).arrayBuffer());
100	
101	    // Compute counter for this chunk's byte offset
102	    const blockOffset = Math.floor(offset / 16);
103	    const counter = new Uint8Array(16);
104	    // Copy first 8 bytes of IV (nonce)
105	    counter.set(iv.subarray(0, AES_CTR_NONCE_SIZE), 0);
106	    // Set bytes 8-15 to blockOffset as big-endian uint64
107	    const dv = new DataView(counter.buffer);
108	    dv.setBigUint64(8, BigInt(blockOffset), false);
109	
110	    const encrypted = await crypto.subtle.encrypt(
111	      { name: AES_CTR_ALGORITHM, counter, length: AES_CTR_LENGTH },
112	      cryptoKey,
113	      chunk
114	    );
115	
116	    encryptedChunks.push(new Uint8Array(encrypted));
117	    offset = end;
118	  }
119	
120	  // 4. Combine encrypted chunks into single Uint8Array
121	  const totalSize = encryptedChunks.reduce((sum, c) => sum + c.length, 0);
122	  const ciphertext = new Uint8Array(totalSize);
123	  let writeOffset = 0;
124	  for (const chunk of encryptedChunks) {
125	    ciphertext.set(chunk, writeOffset);
126	    writeOffset += chunk.length;
127	  }
128	
129	  // 5. Wrap file key with user's public key (ECIES)
130	  const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, userPublicKey);
131	
132	  // 6. Clear sensitive key from memory
133	  clearBytes(fileKey);
134	
135	  return {
136	    ciphertext,
137	    iv: bytesToHex(iv),
138	    wrappedKey: bytesToHex(wrappedKey),
139	    originalSize: file.size,
140	    encryptedSize: file.size, // CTR: same size as plaintext (no auth tag)
141	    encryptionMode: 'CTR' as const,
142	  };
143	}
144	
```

> AGENT

The web app's `selectEncryptionMode` takes a `File` object (browser API), while the centralized one takes `(mimeType, size)`. I'll replace the local function with a thin wrapper that delegates to the shared one:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01WLiTQcL5WXBoougmrayobR
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/services/streaming-crypto.service.ts",
  "old_string": "import type { EncryptedFileResult } from './file-crypto.service';\n\n/** MIME types eligible for CTR streaming encryption */\nconst STREAMING_MIME_TYPES = new Set([\n  'video/mp4',\n  'video/webm',\n  'audio/mpeg',\n  'audio/mp4',\n  'audio/webm',\n  'audio/ogg',\n  'audio/aac',\n  'audio/flac',\n]);\n\n/** Minimum file size (bytes) to use CTR mode: 256KB */\nconst CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD = 256 * 1024;\n\n/** Chunk size for streaming encryption: 1MB */\nconst CHUNK_SIZE = 1024 * 1024;\n\n/** AES-CTR algorithm name */\nconst AES_CTR_ALGORITHM = 'AES-CTR';\n\n/**\n * Select encryption mode based on file MIME type and size.\n *\n * Returns 'CTR' for media files above 256KB (streaming media benefits from\n * random-access decryption). All other files use 'GCM' for authenticated encryption.\n *\n * @param file - File to classify\n * @returns 'CTR' for streaming media, 'GCM' for everything else\n */\nexport function selectEncryptionMode(file: File): 'GCM' | 'CTR' {\n  if (STREAMING_MIME_TYPES.has(file.type) && file.size > CTR_SIZE_THRESHOLD) {\n    return 'CTR';\n  }\n  return 'GCM';\n}",
  "new_string": "import { selectEncryptionMode as selectMode } from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';\nimport type { EncryptedFileResult } from './file-crypto.service';\n\n/** Chunk size for streaming encryption: 1MB */\nconst CHUNK_SIZE = 1024 * 1024;\n\n/** AES-CTR algorithm name */\nconst AES_CTR_ALGORITHM = 'AES-CTR';\n\n/**\n * Select encryption mode based on file MIME type and size.\n *\n * Delegates to the shared policy in @cipherbox/sdk-core.\n */\nexport function selectEncryptionMode(file: File): 'GCM' | 'CTR' {\n  return selectMode(file.type, file.size);\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

Pushed. The CI failure was in `client-upload-concurrency.test.ts` (and would have hit 4 other test files too) — all 5 SDK test files that mock `@cipherbox/sdk-core` were using a bare factory that replaced the entire module, dropping the new `selectEncryptionMode` export. Switched them all to `importOriginal` + spread so real exports survive alongside mocked functions.

LARGE = 512 * 1024; // 512KB — above 256KB threshold\n  const SMALL = 100 * 1024; // 100KB — below threshold\n\n  it('returns CTR for large video/mp4', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('video/mp4', LARGE)).toBe('CTR');\n  });\n\n  it('returns CTR for large video/webm', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('video/webm', LARGE)).toBe('CTR');\n  });\n\n  it('returns CTR for large audio/mpeg', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('audio/mpeg', LARGE)).toBe('CTR');\n  });\n\n  it('returns CTR for large audio/flac', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('audio/flac', LARGE)).toBe('CTR');\n  });\n\n  it('returns GCM for small video (below threshold)', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('video/mp4', SMALL)).toBe('GCM');\n  });\n\n  it('returns GCM for exactly 256KB (not above threshold)', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('video/mp4', 256 * 1024)).toBe('GCM');\n  });\n\n  it('returns CTR for 256KB + 1 byte', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('video/mp4', 256 * 1024 + 1)).toBe('CTR');\n  });\n\n  it('returns GCM for non-media MIME type regardless of size', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('application/pdf', LARGE)).toBe('GCM');\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('text/plain', LARGE)).toBe('GCM');\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('image/png', LARGE)).toBe('GCM');\n  });\n\n  it('returns GCM for unsupported audio MIME type', () => {\n    expect(selectEncryptionMode('audio/wav', LARGE)).toBe('GCM');\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe('normalizeEncryptionMode', () => {\n  it('returns CTR for \"CTR\"', () => {\n    expect(normalizeEncryptionMode('CTR')).toBe('CTR');\n  });\n\n  it('returns GCM for \"GCM\"', () => {\n    expect(normalizeEncryptionMode('GCM')).toBe('GCM');\n  });\n\n  it('returns GCM for undefined', () => {\n    expect(normalizeEncryptionMode(undefined)).toBe('GCM');\n  });\n\n  it('returns GCM for invalid string', () => {\n    expect(normalizeEncryptionMode('INVALID')).toBe('GCM');\n    expect(normalizeEncryptionMode('ctr')).toBe('GCM');\n    expect(normalizeEncryptionMode('')).toBe('GCM');\n  });\n});\n"

10	
11	## Problem
12	
13	BYO-IPFS load tests against staging with Pinata provider: `byo-pin` succeeds (p50=718ms, 20/20 files pinned to Pinata) but `register-cid` and `ipns-publish` both return HTTP 400 from the CipherBox API.
14	
15	The flow is: encrypt file → pin to Pinata → register CID with CipherBox API → publish IPNS. The Pinata pin works, but the CipherBox API rejects the subsequent CID registration.
16	
17	Likely causes:
18	
19	- Test accounts created by load harness may not have BYO mode configured in their vault metadata
20	- Staging API's BYO endpoint may expect parameters the load test workload doesn't send
21	- register-cid endpoint validation may reject CIDs from external providers
22	
23	Discovered during Phase 34 load testing. Pinata JWT auth confirmed working
24	(direct upload test returned 200 with valid CID). Note: the capacity ceiling
25	baselines in `staging-byo-capacity-ceiling.json` show all pins failing with
26	403 -- those were captured while the Pinata free tier was exhausted. After
27	account cleanup, single-client pin succeeded (p50=718ms) but register-cid
28	still returned 400.
29	
30	## Solution
31	
32	1. Check what `register-cid` expects vs what `byo-file-workload.ts` sends — compare request body
33	2. Verify test accounts have BYO provider config in their IPNS metadata (the load harness may skip BYO config seeding)
34	3. Check staging API logs for the 400 error response body to get the specific validation failure
35	4. May need to add BYO config seeding step to `createByoClientPool` in client-pool.ts
36

> AGENT

## Debug BYO-IPFS register-cid 400 errors on staging

**Area:** api
**Created:** 2026-03-29 (18h ago)
**Files:**
- `tests/load/src/workloads/byo-file-workload.ts`
- `packages/sdk-core/src/pinning/pinata-provider.ts`
- `apps/api/src/modules/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts`

### Problem
BYO-IPFS load tests against staging: `byo-pin` succeeds (20/20 files pinned to Pinata, p50=718ms) but `register-cid` and `ipns-publish` both return HTTP 400 from the CipherBox API. Likely the test accounts lack BYO mode config in vault metadata, or the register-cid endpoint validation rejects something the load workload sends.

### Solution
1. Compare what `register-cid` expects vs what the load workload sends
2. Verify test accounts have BYO provider config seeded
3. Check staging API logs for the specific validation failure
4. May need to add BYO config seeding to `createByoClientPool`

```
1	/**
2	 * BYO File Upload/Download Workload
3	 *
4	 * Exercises the full BYO upload path: pin to external provider, register CID
5	 * with CipherBox API, publish IPNS records. Records per-operation metrics
6	 * with byo- prefix to distinguish from CipherBox-only metrics.
7	 *
8	 * Supports two modes:
9	 * - Kubo: direct upload to external node via KuboProvider.pin()
10	 * - PSA: transient relay upload (CipherBox upload -> PSA pinByCid -> CipherBox unpin)
11	 */
12	
13	import type { ByoPoolClient } from '../harness/client-pool';
14	import { PsaProvider } from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
15	
16	export interface ByoFileWorkloadOptions {
17	  /** Number of files to upload */
18	  fileCount: number;
19	  /** Min file size in bytes */
20	  minSize: number;
21	  /** Max file size in bytes */
22	  maxSize: number;
23	  /** Whether to verify downloads after upload (Kubo only; PSA does not support get) */
24	  verifyDownloads: boolean;
25	}
26	
27	/**
28	 * Run a BYO file upload workload on a single client.
29	 *
30	 * Per-file operation sequence (Kubo mode):
31	 *   1. Generate random data (simulates encrypted content)
32	 *   2. Pin to external provider (byo-pin)
33	 *   3. Register CID with CipherBox API (register-cid)
34	 *   4. Publish IPNS record via CipherBox API (ipns-publish)
35	 *   5. Optional: verify download (byo-download)
36	 *   6. Cleanup: unpin from external provider (byo-unpin)
37	 *
38	 * Per-file operation sequence (PSA mode):
39	 *   1. Generate random data
40	 *   2. Upload to CipherBox relay (psa-relay-upload)
41	 *   3. Pin by CID on PSA service (psa-pin-by-cid)
42	 *   4. Unpin from CipherBox relay (psa-relay-unpin)
43	 *   5. Register CID with CipherBox API (register-cid)
44	 *   6. Publish IPNS record via CipherBox API (ipns-publish)
45	 *   7. Cleanup: unpin from PSA (byo-unpin)
46	 */
47	export async function runByoFileWorkload(
48	  pc: ByoPoolClient,
49	  opts: ByoFileWorkloadOptions
50	): Promise<void> {
51	  const { fileCount, minSize, maxSize, verifyDownloads } = opts;
52	  if (minSize < 0 || maxSize < minSize) {
53	    throw new Error(`Invalid size bounds: minSize=${minSize}, maxSize=${maxSize}`);
54	  }
55	
56	  const isPsa = pc.provider instanceof PsaProvider;
57	  const { client, rootIpnsName, metrics } = pc;
58	
59	  for (let i = 0; i < fileCount; i++) {
60	    const size = minSize + Math.floor(Math.random() * (maxSize - minSize + 1));
61	    const data = new Uint8Array(size);
62	    // crypto.getRandomValues() has a 65536 byte limit per call
63	    for (let offset = 0; offset < size; offset += 65536) {
64	      const chunk = new Uint8Array(data.buffer, offset, Math.min(65536, size - offset));
65	      crypto.getRandomValues(chunk);
66	    }
67	
68	    let cid: string | undefined;
69	    let pinSize: number | undefined;
70	    try {
71	      if (isPsa) {
72	        // PSA transient relay flow
73	        // Step 1: Upload to CipherBox to make content available on IPFS network
74	        const relayResult = await metrics.measure(
75	          'psa-relay-upload',
76	          async () => {
77	            const axiosInstance = client.getContext().axiosInstance!;
78	            const blob = new Blob([data as BlobPart]);
79	            const formData = new FormData();
80	            formData.append('file', blob);
81	            const res = await axiosInstance.post<{ cid: string; size: number }>(
82	              '/ipfs/upload',
83	              formData
84	            );
85	            return res.data;
86	          },
87	          size
88	        );
89	
90	        cid = relayResult.cid;
91	        pinSize = relayResult.size;
92	
93	        // Step 2: Pin by CID on PSA service
94	        await metrics.measure('psa-pin-by-cid', async () => {
95	          await (pc.provider as PsaProvider).pinByCid(cid!, `load-${pc.id}-file-${i}`);
96	        });
97	
98	        // Step 3: Unpin from CipherBox relay (content now on PSA service)
99	        await metrics.measure('psa-relay-unpin', async () => {
100	          const axiosInstance = client.getContext().axiosInstance!;
101	          await axiosInstance.post('/ipfs/unpin', { cid });
102	        });
103	      } else {
104	        // Kubo direct upload flow
105	        const pinResult = await metrics.measure(
106	          'byo-pin',
107	          () => pc.provider.pin(data, `load-${pc.id}-file-${i}`),
108	          size
109	        );
110	        cid = pinResult.cid;
111	        pinSize = pinResult.size;
112	      }
113	
114	      // Register CID with CipherBox API (both modes)
115	      // Note: register-cid requires isByoUser flag on vault. If the account
116	      // is not BYO-enabled, this returns 403. We catch and continue so that
117	      // ipns-publish and cleanup still run.
118	      try {
119	        await metrics.measure('register-cid', async () => {
120	          const axiosInstance = client.getContext().axiosInstance!;
121	          await axiosInstance.post('/ipfs/register-cid', { cid, sizeBytes: pinSize });
122	        });
123	      } catch {
124	        // 403 expected for non-BYO accounts -- continue to ipns-publish
125	      }
126	
127	      // Publish IPNS record via CipherBox API (measure raw IPNS API latency)
128	      await metrics.measure('ipns-publish', async () => {
129	        const axiosInstance = client.getContext().axiosInstance!;
130	        // Use publish-batch endpoint with a single record to measure IPNS publish latency
131	        // without coupling to the full upload flow's folder update
132	        await axiosInstance.post('/ipns/publish-batch', {
133	          records: [
134	            {
135	              ipnsName: rootIpnsName,
136	              cid,
137	            },
138	          ],
139	        });
140	      });
141	
142	      // Optional: verify download (Kubo only -- PSA does not support content retrieval)
143	      if (verifyDownloads && !isPsa) {
144	        await metrics.measure(
145	          'byo-download',
146	          async () => {
147	            const downloaded = await pc.provider.get(cid!);
148	            if (downloaded.length !== data.length) {
149	              throw new Error(
150	                `[Client ${pc.id}] Size mismatch: uploaded ${data.length}, downloaded ${downloaded.length}`
151	              );
152	            }
153	            return downloaded;
154	          },
155	          size
156	        );
157	      }
158	    } catch (err) {
159	      console.warn(
160	        `[Client ${pc.id}] BYO file ${i} failed: ${(err as Error).message?.slice(0, 150)}`
161	      );
162	    } finally {
163	      // Cleanup: unpin from external provider whenever we have a CID
164	      // Runs even if later steps (ipns-publish, verify) threw
165	      if (cid) {
166	        try {
167	          await metrics.measure('byo-unpin', () => pc.provider.unpin(cid!));
168	        } catch (cleanupErr) {
169	          console.warn(
170	            `[Client ${pc.id}] Cleanup failed for file ${i}: ${(cleanupErr as Error).message?.slice(0, 150)}`
171	          );
172	        }
173	      }
174	    }
175	  }
176	}
177	
```

Now I understand the flow. The register-cid endpoint:
1. Validates CID format with regex: `^(Qm[1-9A-HJ-NP-Za-km-z]{44,}|b[a-z2-7]{58,})$`
2. Validates sizeBytes is integer 1..100MB
3. Checks `isUserByo` — returns 403 if not BYO

The todo says the error is **400** (validation) not 403 (not BYO). Let me check the load test's client pool to see how CIDs look and if BYO is configured.

1	/**
2	 * Load Test Client Pool
3	 *
4	 * Manages N CipherBoxClient instances, each with its own test account.
5	 * Creates accounts in parallel, distributes workloads, and collects metrics.
6	 *
7	 * Reuses the shared createTestAccount/deleteTestAccount from sdk-e2e
8	 * to avoid duplicating the 5-step account provisioning sequence.
9	 */
10	
11	import {
12	  createTestAccount,
13	  deleteTestAccount,
14	  type TestAccount,
15	} from '../../../sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness';
16	import { MetricsCollector, type OperationMetrics } from './metrics';
17	import { printSummary, toJsonReport } from './reporter';
18	import {
19	  KuboProvider,
20	  PsaProvider,
21	  PinataProvider,
22	  type PinningProvider,
23	  type PinningMode,
24	  type ExternalProviderConfig,
25	  type SdkContext,
26	} from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
27	import { createAxiosInstance, type ApiClientConfig } from '@cipherbox/api-client';
28	
29	const API_URL = process.env.LOAD_TEST_API_URL ?? 'http://localhost:3000';
30	const SECRET = process.env.LOAD_TEST_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production';
31	const THROTTLE_BYPASS = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET ?? '';
32	
33	/** BYO external provider config from environment variables */
34	export const BYO_ENDPOINT = process.env.BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT; // e.g., https://api.pinata.cloud/psa
35	export const BYO_AUTH_TOKEN = process.env.BYO_IPFS_AUTH_TOKEN ?? '';
36	export const BYO_PROTOCOL = (process.env.BYO_IPFS_PROTOCOL ?? 'kubo') as 'kubo' | 'psa' | 'pinata';
37	export const BYO_PROVIDER_NAME = process.env.BYO_IPFS_PROVIDER_NAME ?? 'external';
38	
39	export interface PoolClient extends TestAccount {
40	  id: number;
41	  metrics: MetricsCollector;
42	}
43	
44	export interface ClientPoolOptions {
45	  clientCount: number;
46	  label: string;
47	}
48	
49	/** Options for creating a pool of BYO-mode clients */
50	export interface ByoPoolOptions extends ClientPoolOptions {
51	  /** External provider config -- all BYO clients share this provider */
52	  externalProvider: ExternalProviderConfig;
53	  /** Pinning mode for BYO clients */
54	  pinningMode: 'external' | 'dual';
55	}
56	
57	/** A pool client augmented with BYO provider and mode */
58	export interface ByoPoolClient extends PoolClient {
59	  provider: PinningProvider;
60	  pinningMode: PinningMode;
61	  externalProvider: ExternalProviderConfig;
62	}
63	
64	/**
65	 * Create a pool of N authenticated CipherBoxClient instances.
66	 *
67	 * Each client has its own test account, vault, metrics collector, and
68	 * instance-scoped axios (no shared singleton).
69	 */
70	export async function createClientPool(opts: ClientPoolOptions): Promise<PoolClient[]> {
71	  const { clientCount, label } = opts;
72	  console.log(`Creating ${clientCount} test accounts for "${label}"...`);
73	  const start = performance.now();
74	
75	  // Create accounts in parallel batches of 5 to avoid overwhelming the API
76	  const clients: PoolClient[] = [];
77	  const batchSize = 5;
78	
79	  for (let batch = 0; batch < clientCount; batch += batchSize) {
80	    const batchEnd = Math.min(batch + batchSize, clientCount);
81	    const promises = [];
82	
83	    for (let i = batch; i < batchEnd; i++) {
84	      promises.push(
85	        createTestAccount({
86	          apiUrl: API_URL,
87	          secret: SECRET,
88	          label: `${label}-${i}`,
89	          emailPrefix: 'load',
90	        }).then((account): PoolClient => ({ ...account, id: i, metrics: new MetricsCollector() }))
91	      );
92	    }
93	
94	    const results = await Promise.allSettled(promises);
95	    for (const result of results) {
96	      if (result.status === 'fulfilled') {
97	        clients.push(result.value);
98	      } else {
99	        console.warn(`Failed to create pool client: ${result.reason}`);
100	      }
101	    }
102	  }
103	
104	  const elapsed = performance.now() - start;
105	  console.log(
106	    `Created ${clients.length}/${clientCount} clients in ${(elapsed / 1000).toFixed(1)}s`
107	  );
108	
109	  return clients;
110	}
111	
112	/**
113	 * Destroy all clients in the pool and delete test accounts.
114	 */
115	export async function destroyClientPool(pool: PoolClient[]): Promise<void> {
116	  console.log(`Cleaning up ${pool.length} test accounts...`);
117	  for (const pc of pool) {
118	    pc.client.destroy();
119	    // Zero harness-owned key material (client.destroy() only clears its internal copies)
120	    pc.privateKey.fill(0);
121	    pc.rootFolderKey.fill(0);
122	    pc.rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey.fill(0);
123	    await deleteTestAccount(pc, API_URL);
124	  }
125	}
126	
127	/**
128	 * Create a pool of N BYO-mode authenticated CipherBoxClient instances.
129	 *
130	 * Each client has its own test account plus a PinningProvider instance
131	 * (KuboProvider or PsaProvider) for external pinning.
132	 *
133	 * Returns an empty array (with warning) if BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT is not set,
134	 * allowing scenarios to skip gracefully.
135	 */
136	export async function createByoClientPool(opts: ByoPoolOptions): Promise<ByoPoolClient[]> {
137	  if (!BYO_ENDPOINT) {
138	    console.warn(
139	      'BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT not set -- skipping BYO pool creation. ' +
140	        'Set BYO_IPFS_ENDPOINT, BYO_IPFS_AUTH_TOKEN, and optionally BYO_IPFS_PROTOCOL to run BYO scenarios.'
141	    );
142	    return [];
143	  }
144	
145	  const basePool = await createClientPool({
146	    clientCount: opts.clientCount,
147	    label: opts.label,
148	  });
149	
150	  const byoPool: ByoPoolClient[] = basePool.map((pc) => {
151	    let provider: PinningProvider;
152	    switch (opts.externalProvider.protocol) {
153	      case 'psa':
154	        provider = new PsaProvider(opts.externalProvider.endpoint, opts.externalProvider.authToken);
155	        break;
156	      case 'pinata':
157	        provider = new PinataProvider(
158	          opts.externalProvider.endpoint,
159	          opts.externalProvider.authToken
160	        );
161	        break;
162	      case 'kubo':
163	      default:
164	        provider = new KuboProvider(
165	          opts.externalProvider.endpoint,
166	          opts.externalProvider.authToken
167	        );
168	        break;
169	    }
170	
171	    return {
172	      ...pc,
173	      provider,
174	      pinningMode: opts.pinningMode,
175	      externalProvider: opts.externalProvider,
176	    };
177	  });
178	
179	  console.log(
180	    `BYO pool: ${byoPool.length} clients with ${opts.externalProvider.protocol} provider ` +
181	      `(mode: ${opts.pinningMode}, endpoint: ${opts.externalProvider.endpoint})`
182	  );
183	
184	  return byoPool;
185	}
186	
187	/**
188	 * Re-authenticate a test account via /auth/test-login.
189	 * Returns the new access token.
190	 */
191	async function reAuthenticate(email: string): Promise<string> {
192	  const headers: Record<string, string> = { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' };
193	  if (THROTTLE_BYPASS) {
194	    headers['X-Throttle-Bypass'] = THROTTLE_BYPASS;
195	  }
196	
197	  const res = await fetch(`${API_URL}/auth/test-login`, {
198	    method: 'POST',
199	    headers,
200	    body: JSON.stringify({ email, secret: SECRET }),
201	  });
202	
203	  if (!res.ok) {
204	    throw new Error(`Re-auth failed (${res.status}): ${await res.text()}`);
205	  }
206	
207	  const { accessToken } = (await res.json()) as { accessToken: string };
208	  return accessToken;
209	}
210	
211	/**
212	 * Create an SdkContext for headless sdk-core calls from a PoolClient.
213	 *
214	 * Constructs a fresh axiosInstance with 401 interceptor so that
215	 * headless workloads get automatic token refresh.
216	 */
217	export function createSdkContext(pc: PoolClient): SdkContext {
218	  // Mutable token holder -- updated by 401 interceptor
219	  let currentToken = pc.accessToken;
220	
221	  // Shared refresh promise to coalesce concurrent 401 re-auths
222	  let refreshPromise: Promise<string> | null = null;
223	
224	  const config: ApiClientConfig = {
225	    baseUrl: API_URL,
226	    getAccessToken: async () => currentToken,
227	    refreshAccessToken: async () => {
228	      if (!refreshPromise) {
229	        refreshPromise = reAuthenticate(pc.email).finally(() => {
230	          refreshPromise = null;
231	        });
232	      }
233	      const newToken = await refreshPromise;
234	      currentToken = newToken;
235	      pc.accessToken = newToken;
236	      return newToken;
237	    },
238	    defaultHeaders: THROTTLE_BYPASS ? { 'X-Throttle-Bypass': THROTTLE_BYPASS } : undefined,
239	  };
240	
241	  const axiosInstance = createAxiosInstance(config);
242	
243	  return {
244	    apiUrl: API_URL,
245	    getAccessToken: async () => currentToken,
246	    axiosInstance,
247	  };
248	}
249	
250	/**
251	 * Aggregate metrics from all pool clients, print summary, and return metrics.
252	 * Extracts the repeated boilerplate from every load test scenario.
253	 */
254	export async function aggregateAndReport(
255	  scenarioName: string,
256	  pool: PoolClient[]
257	): Promise<OperationMetrics[]> {
258	  const allMetrics = new MetricsCollector();
259	
260	  let minTimestamp = Infinity;
261	  let maxTimestamp = 0;
262	
263	  for (const pc of pool) {
264	    for (const sample of pc.metrics.getReadonlySamples()) {
265	      allMetrics.record(sample);
266	      if (sample.timestamp < minTimestamp) minTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
267	      if (sample.timestamp > maxTimestamp) maxTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
268	    }
269	  }
270	
271	  // Set collector timing from sample timestamps for accurate throughput calculation
272	  allMetrics.setElapsedMs(maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 1);
273	
274	  const totalDuration = maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 0;
275	  const metrics = allMetrics.getMetrics();
276	
277	  printSummary(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
278	
279	  // Write JSON report to file for CI artifact upload
280	  const reportJson = toJsonReport(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
281	  console.log('\nJSON Report:\n' + reportJson);
282	
283	  try {

1	import type { PinningProvider, PinResult, PinStatus } from './types';
2	
3	/** Fixed upload URL for Pinata file uploads (always this domain, not configurable) */
4	const UPLOAD_URL = 'https://uploads.pinata.cloud';
5	
6	/** Timeout for all Pinata API requests — higher ceiling for external network uploads */
7	const REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000;
8	
9	/**
10	 * PinningProvider implementation for Pinata's native v3 API.
11	 *
12	 * Pinata deprecated their PSA endpoint (/psa returns 404). Their current API
13	 * uses proprietary endpoints:
14	 * - /v3/files for upload and management
15	 * - /pinning/pinByHash for CID-based pinning
16	 *
17	 * Two base URLs are used:
18	 * - https://uploads.pinata.cloud -- file uploads (fixed, not configurable)
19	 * - https://api.pinata.cloud -- management operations (list, delete, pinByHash)
20	 *
21	 * The `endpoint` constructor param should be the management URL
22	 * (https://api.pinata.cloud).
23	 */
24	export class PinataProvider implements PinningProvider {
25	  private readonly endpoint: string;
26	  private readonly authToken: string;
27	  private readonly gatewayUrl?: string;
28	
29	  constructor(endpoint: string, authToken: string, gatewayUrl?: string) {
30	    this.endpoint = endpoint.replace(/\/+$/, '');
31	    this.authToken = authToken;
32	    this.gatewayUrl = gatewayUrl?.replace(/\/+$/, '');
33	  }
34	
35	  /**
36	   * Upload and pin data via Pinata's v3 file upload API.
37	   * POST https://uploads.pinata.cloud/v3/files
38	   */
39	  async pin(data: Uint8Array, name?: string): Promise<PinResult> {
40	    const blob = new Blob([data as BlobPart], { type: 'application/octet-stream' });
41	    const formData = new FormData();
42	    formData.append('file', blob, name ?? `cipherbox-${Date.now()}`);
43	
44	    const response = await fetch(`${UPLOAD_URL}/v3/files`, {
45	      method: 'POST',
46	      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${this.authToken}` },
47	      body: formData,
48	      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),
49	    });
50	
51	    if (!response.ok) {
52	      const errorText = await response.text();
53	      throw new Error(`Pinata upload failed: ${response.status} - ${errorText}`);
54	    }
55	
56	    const result = (await response.json()) as {
57	      data: { id: string; cid: string; size: number };
58	    };
59	    return { cid: result.data.cid, size: result.data.size };
60	  }
61	
62	  /**
63	   * Pin an existing CID via Pinata's pinByHash endpoint.
64	   * Pinata will retrieve the content from the IPFS network and pin it.
65	   * POST https://api.pinata.cloud/pinning/pinByHash
66	   */
67	  async pinByCid(cid: string): Promise<{ cid: string; status: string }> {
68	    const response = await fetch(`${this.endpoint}/pinning/pinByHash`, {
69	      method: 'POST',
70	      headers: {
71	        Authorization: `Bearer ${this.authToken}`,
72	        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
73	      },
74	      body: JSON.stringify({ hashToPin: cid }),
75	      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),
76	    });
77	
78	    if (!response.ok) {
79	      const errorText = await response.text();
80	      throw new Error(`Pinata pinByHash failed: ${response.status} - ${errorText}`);
81	    }
82	
83	    const result = (await response.json()) as { ipfsHash: string; status: string };
84	    return { cid: result.ipfsHash, status: result.status };
85	  }
86	
87	  /**
88	   * Remove a pin by CID.
89	   * Pinata uses file IDs for deletion, so we first look up the file(s)
90	   * by CID, then delete each one.
91	   */
92	  async unpin(cid: string): Promise<void> {
93	    const listResponse = await fetch(`${this.endpoint}/v3/files?cid=${encodeURIComponent(cid)}`, {
94	      method: 'GET',
95	      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${this.authToken}` },
96	      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),
97	    });
98	
99	    if (!listResponse.ok) {
100	      throw new Error(`Pinata list failed: ${listResponse.status}`);
101	    }
102	
103	    const listResult = (await listResponse.json()) as {
104	      data: { files: Array<{ id: string }> };
105	    };
106	
107	    for (const file of listResult.data.files) {
108	      const deleteResponse = await fetch(`${this.endpoint}/v3/files/${file.id}`, {
109	        method: 'DELETE',
110	        headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${this.authToken}` },
111	        signal: AbortSignal.timeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),
112	      });
113	      if (!deleteResponse.ok) {
114	        throw new Error(`Pinata delete failed: ${deleteResponse.status}`);
115	      }
116	    }
117	  }
118	
119	  /**
120	   * Check pin status by CID.
121	   * Queries the Pinata v3 files API and returns 'pinned' if found, 'failed' if not.
122	   */
123	  async status(cid: string): Promise<PinStatus> {
124	    const response = await fetch(`${this.endpoint}/v3/files?cid=${encodeURIComponent(cid)}`, {
125	      method: 'GET',
126	      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${this.authToken}` },
127	      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),
128	    });
129	
130	    if (!response.ok) return { cid, status: 'failed' };
131	
132	    const result = (await response.json()) as {
133	      data: { files: Array<{ id: string; cid: string }> };
134	    };
135	
136	    return {
137	      cid,
138	      status: result.data.files.length > 0 ? 'pinned' : 'failed',
139	    };
140	  }
141	
142	  /**
143	   * Fetch pinned content by CID via Pinata's gateway.
144	   * Uses the dedicated gateway URL if configured, otherwise the default public gateway.
145	   */
146	  async get(cid: string): Promise<Uint8Array> {
147	    const gateway = this.gatewayUrl ?? 'https://gateway.pinata.cloud';
148	    const response = await fetch(`${gateway}/ipfs/${encodeURIComponent(cid)}`, {
149	      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),
150	    });
151	
152	    if (!response.ok) {
153	      throw new Error(`Pinata gateway fetch failed: ${response.status}`);
154	    }
155	
156	    return new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer());
157	  }
158	}

> AGENT

Found both bugs. The load test's `ipns-publish` call on line 132-138 sends:

```js
{ records: [{ ipnsName: rootIpnsName, cid }] }
```

But `PublishIpnsEntryDto` requires `record` (base64 IPNS record) and `metadataCid` — not `cid`. The field name is wrong and the signed IPNS record is missing entirely.

For `register-cid`, the issue is that `createByoClientPool` never enables BYO mode on the accounts. The todo says 400, but looking at the code the first failure would actually be on the DTO validation (if size is wrong) or 403 from the BYO check. Let me check if there's a `setByo` API endpoint.

> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. There are **two bugs** in the load test workload:

1. **`register-cid` → 400/403**: `createByoClientPool` never enables BYO mode on test accounts (no API call to set `isByoUser = true`). Since `setByoStatus` isn't exposed via an endpoint, the load test needs to use a direct DB path or a new endpoint. Actually — looking more carefully, the real SDK `uploadFile` flow with BYO triggers `registerCid` via the `DualPinProvider`. The load test bypasses the SDK entirely and calls the raw API. The fix: add a vault endpoint to toggle BYO, OR have the load test call the settings-save endpoint. Let me check if there's a settings endpoint.

> AGENT

Confirmed: **`setByoStatus` is a dead code path** — the service method exists but there's no API endpoint and nothing calls it. This means:

1. `register-cid` always returns **403** (not 400 as the todo says) because `isByoUser` is always `false`
2. The load test's `ipns-publish` returns **400** because it sends `{ ipnsName, cid }` instead of `{ ipnsName, record, metadataCid }`

The real fix has two parts:

**Bug 1**: Add a `PATCH /vault/byo-status` endpoint so BYO mode can be toggled.
**Bug 2**: Fix the load test's `ipns-publish` call (it sends wrong fields — but this is a load test issue, not a product bug).

For the load test, `register-cid` and `ipns-publish` are measuring raw API latency outside the SDK — they don't need to send valid IPNS records. The fix should either:
- Have `createByoClientPool` enable BYO mode via the new endpoint
- Fix the ipns-publish request to match the DTO

Let me implement the API endpoint first, then fix the load test.

Now fix the ipns-publish call in the workload. The load test is measuring raw IPNS API latency — it doesn't have a real signed record. The simplest fix: skip the ipns-publish step since it can't produce a valid signed IPNS record without the full crypto pipeline, and the metrics it was meant to capture (IPNS publish latency) are already measured by the main upload flow in other scenarios.

Actually, let me reconsider. The point of the BYO workload is to measure the full BYO pipeline end-to-end. The `ipns-publish` step needs a valid signed IPNS record. The SDK creates these. Let me check if the load test has access to the SDK client's IPNS publish path.

Looking at the workload: `pc.client` is a `CipherBoxClient`. The SDK's `uploadFile()` already does IPNS publish internally. The load test is decomposing the pipeline into separate measurable steps. For `ipns-publish`, it needs a real record.

The simplest fix: use `sdk-core`'s `createAndPublishIpnsRecord` which handles record creation + publish. The load test has `rootIpnsName` and the account has `rootIpnsKeypair`.

> AGENT

The issue is that `republishService` is typed as `jest.Mocked<Partial<RepublishService>>` but `module.get(RepublishService)` returns `RepublishService`, so `.getHealthStats` is not recognized as a mock. The fix: cast at line 24.

1	# Agent Learnings
2	
3	This folder contains learnings from AI-assisted development sessions. Each entry captures insights that help future sessions work more effectively in this codebase.
4	
5	## When to Create an Entry
6	
7	Create an entry after completing a significant task that involved:
8	- Learning something non-obvious about the codebase
9	- Getting stuck and finding a solution
10	- Discovering patterns or gotchas worth remembering
11	
12	## File Naming
13	
14	`YYYY-MM-DD-brief-description.md`
15	
16	Example: `2026-02-06-discord-style-usernames.md`
17	
18	## Entry Structure
19	
20	```markdown
21	# [Brief Title]
22	
23	**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD
24	
25	## Original Prompt
26	
27	> [Full prompt text as provided by user - preserving exact wording helps improve future prompts]
28	
29	## What I Learned
30	
31	Bullet points of non-obvious discoveries:
32	- Gotchas and edge cases
33	- Codebase patterns
34	- Data structure quirks
35	- Things that look similar but behave differently
36	
37	## What Would Have Helped
38	
39	Information that would have made the task smoother if known upfront:
40	- Missing context from the prompt
41	- Files I should have checked first
42	- Clarifying questions I should have asked
43	
44	## Key Files
45	
46	List of files most relevant to this type of task (for future reference).
47	```
48	
49	## Guidelines
50	
51	- **Be concise** - Future you wants quick insights, not a novel
52	- **Focus on learnings** - Skip implementation details that are obvious from the code
53	- **Highlight gotchas** - The non-obvious stuff is most valuable
54	- **Think reusability** - What would help with similar tasks in the future?
55	
```
