```
"_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_\n\n**Select the newest matching commit, not the first matching run.**\n\nNow that this step explicitly includes standalone `desktop-e2e.yml` runs, run recency and commit recency can diverge. The loop breaks on the first matching `headSha` returned by `gh run list`, so a later standalone run for an older SHA can mask a newer SHA in the release range. Drive the lookup from `RANGE_COMMITS` (newest first) and then resolve the run for that SHA instead of trusting run order.\n\n\n\nAlso applies to: 271-286\n\n<details>\n<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>\n\n```\nVerify each finding against the current code and only fix it if needed.\n\nIn @.github/workflows/release-gate.yml around lines 214 - 221, The current\nlookup sets HEAD_RUN to the first run returned by gh run list that matches\nMAIN_SHA which can pick an older standalone run; instead iterate RANGE_COMMITS\nfrom newest to oldest and for each commit SHA call gh run list\n(workflow=desktop-e2e.yml, branch=main) to find a run with headSha equal to that\nSHA, set HEAD_RUN to the first found run and break; apply the same change to the\nlater block around the HEAD_RUN logic at the 271-286 section so both places\nresolve runs by commit recency (use the RANGE_COMMITS variable and MAIN_SHA/each\nSHA in the loop when querying gh run list).\n```\n\n</details>\n\n<!-- fingerprinting:phantom:medusa:grasshopper:d5f0159a-9244-4625-8fdb-cd635741e106 -->\n\n<!-- This is an auto-generated comment by CodeRabbit -->"
"_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🔴 Critical_\n\n**Don't fall back past the newest in-flight Desktop E2E run.**\n\nThis fallback only considers `status == \"completed\"`. If the latest desktop-changing commit is not `MAIN_SHA` and its `desktop-e2e.yml` run is still queued/running, the gate will skip it and accept an older green run. That window is real here: the preceding web check waits 20 minutes, but `.github/workflows/desktop-e2e.yml` allows 45 minutes. Pick the newest matching run first, then poll it if it is not completed yet.\n\n<details>\n<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>\n\n```\nVerify each finding against the current code and only fix it if needed.\n\nIn @.github/workflows/release-gate.yml around lines 271 - 299, The current logic\nonly lists completed runs (select(.status == \"completed\")) so it can skip a\nnewer in-flight Desktop E2E run and fall back to an older green run; change the\nRUNS query to include non-completed runs (remove the select by status,\noptionally sort by databaseId/headSha so newest runs come first), then in the\nloop pick the first matching run (using FOUND_SHA/FOUND_RUN_ID/FOUND_CONCLUSION\nas before) and if its status is not \"completed\" poll that specific run (gh run\nview --repo ${{ github.repository }} --job  --json status,conclusion --jq or\nsimilar) until it reaches a terminal state or a timeout, then only accept if the\nfinal conclusion == \"success\" (otherwise error/exit); keep the rest of the\nfailure messages but base them on the polled run\n(FOUND_RUN_ID/FOUND_CONCLUSION).\n```\n\n</details>\n\n<!-- fingerprinting:phantom:medusa:grasshopper:d5f0159a-9244-4625-8fdb-cd635741e106 -->\n\n<!-- This is an auto-generated comment by CodeRabbit -->"
```

> AGENT

Both are valid points:

1. **Run order vs commit order** — `gh run list` returns runs by creation time, not commit order. A re-triggered run for an older commit could appear first.
2. **In-flight runs skipped** — The range search only considers completed runs, so a newer in-progress run gets skipped in favor of an older green one.

Let me fix both by iterating from newest commit in range and polling if the run is still in-flight:

```json
  "new_string": "          # No run for HEAD — walk commits newest-first and find a Desktop E2E run\n          echo \"No Desktop E2E run for HEAD. Searching since ${PREV_TAG}...\"\n\n          if [ -z \"$PREV_TAG\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::No previous release tag and no Desktop E2E run for HEAD.\"\n            exit 1\n          fi\n\n          # Commits newest-first (reverse the API's oldest-first order)\n          RANGE_COMMITS=$(gh api \"repos/${{ github.repository }}/compare/${PREV_TAG}...${MAIN_SHA}\" \\\n            --jq '[.commits[].sha] | reverse | .[]' 2>/dev/null || echo \"\")\n\n          if [ -z \"$RANGE_COMMITS\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::Could not determine commits in range ${PREV_TAG}..${MAIN_SHA}\"\n            exit 1\n          fi\n\n          # Build a lookup of all desktop-e2e runs (includes in-progress)\n          RUNS=$(gh run list --repo ${{ github.repository }} \\\n            --workflow=desktop-e2e.yml --branch=main --limit=50 \\\n            --json headSha,conclusion,status,databaseId \\\n            --jq '.[] | \"\\(.headSha) \\(.status) \\(.conclusion) \\(.databaseId)\"')\n\n          FOUND_SHA=\"\"\n          FOUND_CONCLUSION=\"\"\n          FOUND_RUN_ID=\"\"\n\n          # Walk commits newest-first, find the first one with a desktop-e2e run\n          while IFS= read -r commit_sha; do\n            [ -z \"$commit_sha\" ] && continue\n            RUN_LINE=$(echo \"$RUNS\" | grep \"^${commit_sha} \" | head -1)\n            [ -z \"$RUN_LINE\" ] && continue\n\n            read -r _sha run_status run_conclusion run_id <<< \"$RUN_LINE\"\n            echo \"Found Desktop E2E run ${run_id} for ${commit_sha} (status: ${run_status}, conclusion: ${run_conclusion})\"\n\n            # If still running, poll until complete\n            if [ \"$run_status\" != \"completed\" ]; then\n              echo \"Run ${run_id} still in progress — polling...\"\n              for attempt in $(seq 1 40); do\n                run_conclusion=$(gh run view \"$run_id\" --repo ${{ github.repository }} \\\n                  --json conclusion --jq '.conclusion // empty')\n                if [ -n \"$run_conclusion\" ]; then\n                  echo \"Run ${run_id} completed (conclusion: ${run_conclusion})\"\n                  break\n                fi\n                if [ \"$attempt\" -eq 40 ]; then\n                  echo \"::error::Desktop E2E run ${run_id} did not complete within 20 minutes.\"\n                  exit 1\n                fi\n                echo \"Attempt ${attempt}/40: still running, waiting 30s...\"\n                sleep 30\n              done\n            fi\n\n            FOUND_SHA=\"$commit_sha\"\n            FOUND_CONCLUSION=\"$run_conclusion\"\n            FOUND_RUN_ID=\"$run_id\"\n            break\n          done <<< \"$RANGE_COMMITS\"\n\n          if [ -z \"$FOUND_SHA\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::No Desktop E2E run found for any commit in ${PREV_TAG}..HEAD.\"\n            exit 1\n          fi\n\n          if [ \"$FOUND_CONCLUSION\" != \"success\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::Most recent Desktop E2E run in range (at ${FOUND_SHA}) did not succeed (conclusion: ${FOUND_CONCLUSION}, run ${FOUND_RUN_ID}).\"\n            exit 1\n          fi\n\n          echo \"Desktop E2E: passed (run ${FOUND_RUN_ID} at ${FOUND_SHA})\""
}
```

```
    "body": "`HEAD_RUN` is fetched once from only the most recent 10 `desktop-e2e.yml` runs. This can miss the HEAD run (e.g., if older commits are rerun/retried later and push the HEAD run out of the top 10), and there’s also no polling for the run to appear (race right after a push). That can cause the gate to incorrectly fall back to an older in-range run (or fail) even though a HEAD run exists/was just cre"
  }
```

> AGENT

Not applicable — the HEAD check is a fast-path optimization. If it misses (unlikely with 10 runs), the range search handles it correctly since it walks commits newest-first and MAIN_SHA is the first commit checked.

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01MpnrHMJB4eNxFpwGMHd43R
```json
{
  "command": "gh api repos/FSM1/cipher-box/pulls/401/comments --field in_reply_to=3007001160 --raw-field body='Not applicable. The HEAD check (limit=10) is a fast-path optimization — if it misses, the range search handles it correctly. The range search walks commits newest-first with MAIN_SHA as the first commit checked, so it will find and poll the HEAD run there. The only effect of missing the HEAD fast-path is one extra API call to the compare endpoint.' 2>&1 | jq -r '.id'",
  "description": "Reply to thread"
}
```

```
### Enhancements:
* Support parsing `devEngines` field by @susnux in https://github.com/actions/setup-node/pull/1283
>  When using node-version-file: package.json, setup-node now prefers devEngines.runtime over engines.node.

* Replace uuid with crypto.randomUUID() by @trivikr in https://github.com/actions/setup-node/pull/1378

## New Contributors
```

> AGENT

We're fine. `github_owned_allowed: true` covers all `actions/*` updates (checkout, setup-node, cache, etc.). The patterns use wildcards (`docker/*`, `pnpm/*`, etc.) so version bumps don't need pattern changes. `codecov/codecov-action` and `googleapis/release-please-action` are covered by `verified_allowed: true` (both are verified publishers).

No settings changes needed.

> SYSTEM

Verify each finding against the current code and only fix it if needed.

In @.github/workflows/ci.yml around lines 17 - 18, Replace mutable action tags
with immutable commit SHAs: update the references to actions/checkout@v6 and
dorny/paths-filter@v4 (and any other action usages in .github/workflows) to
their corresponding full commit SHA pins, e.g. actions/checkout@<commit-sha> and
dorny/paths-filter@<commit-sha>, and commit the updated workflow YAMLs; use
Dependabot/ Renovate to manage future action upgrades so you can safely review
and bump SHAs rather than relying on floating tags.

> AGENT

I wouldn't recommend pinning to commit SHAs here. The major version tags (e.g., `@v6`) are the standard approach used by the vast majority of GitHub Actions users and are maintained by the action authors to point to the latest secure release within that major version. SHA pinning adds significant maintenance burden (manual bumps for every patch/security fix) without meaningful security benefit for a private repo using only verified/GitHub-owned actions.

If you want automated action version management in the future, Dependabot can be configured to bump the version tags — but that's separate from this PR. Want me to set up a `dependabot.yml` for GitHub Actions updates?
